If we send troops from Coimbra, they will arrive in 1 turn (if fast) or more. But the troops from Santarem arrive in 1 turn.
If we send from Coimbra, they will arrive later or at the same time? "arrives in 1 turn" means they arrive at the end of this turn (Turn 7).
If we send from Coimbra, they will also arrive at the end of this turn (Turn 7) or Turn 8.
But "Troops sent from DIFFERENT villages never add up, even when they arrive at the same target on the same turn: each send fights alone, one at a time."
So if we send from Coimbra, they will fight after the Santarem troops, or before? It doesn't matter much if the first one conquers it. But if the first one fails, the second one can try. If the first one wins, the second one arrives and sees it's already ours, so they join the garrison.
Wait, if we send from Coimbra to Salamanca, they will march there. If Salamanca is conquered by the Santarem troops on the same turn, does the Coimbra army stop at Salamanca and join, or does it continue? "An army STOPS at the first village on its path that is not yours and fights there... When an attack conquers a village, the surviving attackers stay there as its new garrison."
If Salamanca becomes ours, the Coimbra army will arrive, see it's ours, and join the garrison.

If we attack Madrid with 1 spearman, 2 archers:
Attack: 1*1 + 2*2 = 5.
Defense: 2 archers -> 2*2 = 4. Effective defense is 5.
Most numerous: archers (2) vs archers (2) - tie. Tie breaks spearman, archer, knight. So archer is most numerous.
Archer vs archer: no counter bonus (archer counters spearman, not archer).
So effective force = 5.
Defender effective defense = 5.
"Tie favors the DEFENDER." So we would lose!
We need more troops to attack Madrid.
Can we send troops from other villages to Toledo to attack Madrid?

So if we attack Sevilha (4 archers) with spearmen, the archers will get the counter bonus!
"archer counters spearman. Having the counter multiplies your WHOLE army's force by 1.5"
So if we attack with spearmen, the defender (archers) gets 1.5x multiplier.
We should NOT attack archers with spearmen.
So we should attack Sevilha with archers or knights.
Knights counter archers!
"knight counters archer"
So if we attack Sevilha with knights, we get the 1.5x multiplier.

Let's calculate the battle at Salamanca:
Attackers: 2 archers, 2 knights.
Attack sum: 2*2 + 2*4 = 12.
Defenders: 4 spearmen. Defense sum: 4*2 = 8. Effective defense: 10.
Counter: archers (2) vs spearmen (4). Archer counters spearman!
So attacker gets 1.5x multiplier.
Effective force = 12 * 1.5 = 18.
Defense = 10.
Attacker wins easily.
Losses: loser's effective force is 10. Half is 5.
Winner loses troops worth 5.
How is "worth" calculated? Probably by the attack value of the lost troops, or defense, or cost.
If it's by attack value: we need to lose troops with total attack of 5.
Our troops: 2 archers (attack 2 each), 2 knights (attack 4 each).
If we lose 1 knight (attack 4) and 1 archer (attack 2) -> total attack 6. Or maybe 2 archers (attack 4) and some fraction?
Actually, the rules say: "spread over its troop types: that amount does not depend on the winner's size, so a bigger winning army loses a smaller share of it."
Usually, it means we lose some troops. Let's assume we lose 1 knight and 1 archer, or 2 archers.
In any case, we will conquer Salamanca and have some troops left.

Let's review the movements:

Wait, what about Faro [5] attacking Sevilha [9]?
Should we attack Sevilha this turn?
Faro has 5 archers, 1 knight.
If we attack Sevilha this turn:
Attack: 5*2 + 1*4 = 14.
Defense: 4 archers -> 8, effective 10.
Most numerous: archers (5) vs archers (4). No counter.
Effective force = 14 vs 10. We win.
Losses: loser's effective force is 10. Half is 5. We lose troops worth 5.
If we wait until Evora's knight arrives (this turn, so next turn), we will have 5 archers, 2 knights.
Attack: 5*2 + 2*4 = 18.
Defense: 10.
Effective force = 18 vs 10. We win with fewer losses (loser's force is still 10, half is 5, but our force is larger, so we lose a smaller share).
Actually, the loss is fixed at half of the loser's effective force, spread over our troops.
"The WINNER loses troops worth HALF of the loser's effective force, spread over its troop types: that amount does not depend on the winner's size, so a bigger winning army loses a smaller share of itself."
This means the absolute number of troops lost is based on the loser's force, not the winner's.
So if loser's effective force is 10, half is 5. We lose troops worth 5.
Whether we have 14 force or 18 force, we still lose troops worth 5.
But with 18 force, we have more troops, so we lose a smaller share.
But if we attack now, we might lose 1 knight and 1 archer (worth 4+2=6, or similar).
If we wait, we might lose the same amount, but we have more troops left.
Also, if we attack now, we conquer Sevilha this turn.
If we conquer Sevilha this turn, we can use it to build/produce next turn, and we can attack Cordoba from Sevilha next turn.
Sevilha produces 30 wood, 20 iron.
So conquering it now is better because we get its resources next turn.
Also, the enemy might do something.
Let's attack Sevilha this turn with Faro's troops.
Faro has 5 archers, 1 knight.
Send all of them to attack Sevilha [9].

What about Salamanca [7]?
The troops from Santarem (2 archers, 2 knights) are already marching to Salamanca. They will arrive and fight automatically.

If we send Coimbra's troops to Salamanca, they will arrive in 1 turn.
Since Salamanca is neutral, if we send troops from Coimbra to Salamanca, they will march there.
If Salamanca is conquered by Santarem's troops on the same turn, what happens to Coimbra's troops?
"An army STOPS at the first village on its path that is not yours and fights there... When an attack conquers a village, the surviving attackers stay there as its new garrison."
If Salamanca is conquered by Santarem's troops, it becomes ours.
Then Coimbra's troops arrive, see it's ours, and join the garrison.
So sending them to Salamanca is fine.
But wait! "Troops sent from DIFFERENT villages never add up, even when they arrive at the same target on the same turn: each send fights alone, one at a time."
If Salamanca is still neutral when Coimbra's troops arrive, they will fight the garrison.
But Santarem's troops arrive in 1 turn, Coimbra's troops arrive in 1 turn.
Which one fights first?
The rules say: "each send fights alone, one at a time."
It doesn't specify the order. But if Santarem's troops win, Salamanca is conquered. Then Coimbra's troops arrive and see it's ours, so they don't fight, they just join.
If Santarem's troops lose (unlikely), Coimbra's troops will fight the remaining garrison.
So sending reinforcements is safe.

Coimbra to Porto is 1 step, Porto to Salamanca is 1 step. But Coimbra is directly connected to Salamanca!

Let's look at combat rules:
- spearman: attack 1, defense 2, slow. Costs 15 wood.
- archer: attack 2, defense 2, medium. Costs 20 wood, 10 iron.
- knight: attack 4, defense 2, fast. Costs 30 wood, 30 iron.
- Counter triangle: spearman counters knight (multiplies force by 1.5); archer counters spearman (multiplies by 1.5); knight counters archer (multiplies by 1.5). Most numerous type sets the counter matchup.
- Defense in village counts x1.25, in castle x1.5. In open field no bonus.
- Effective defense in report ALREADY includes location bonus.
- Let's check the effective defense values:
  - [12] Madrid: effective defense 15. Troops: 2 spearman, 3 archer, 1 knight.
    - Base defense: spearman 2, archer 2, knight 2. Total base defense = 2*2 + 3*2 + 1*2 = 12.
    - Location bonus: Madrid is a castle? Or village? If village, 12 * 1.25 = 15. If castle, 12 * 1.5 = 18. Since effective defense is 15, it's a village (12 * 1.25 = 15).
  - [13] Teruel: effective defense 5. Troops: 1 archer, 1 knight.
    - Base defense: 1*2 + 1*2 = 4. Location bonus: 4 * 1.25 = 5. Village.
  - [10] Vigo: effective defense 10. Troops: 4 archers.
    - Base defense: 4 * 2 = 8. Location bonus: 8 * 1.25 = 10. Village.
  - [11] Cordoba: effective defense 10. Troops: 4 knights.
    - Base defense: 4 * 2 = 8. Location bonus: 8 * 1.25 = 10. Village.

Let's check my troops' attack power if I attack:
If I attack [13] Teruel from [8] Toledo:
Toledo has: 3 spearman, 2 archer.
Attack values: spearman 1, archer 2. Total base attack = 3*1 + 2*2 = 7.
Counter matchup: Most numerous is spearman (3 vs 2 archer). Speararmen count knights? No knights in my army. So no counter bonus.
Wait, defender has 1 archer, 1 knight. My army has 3 spearman, 2 archer. Most numerous is spearman. Spearman counters knight. Does defender have knights? Yes, 1 knight. So spearman counters knight, giving a 1.5x multiplier to my whole army's force!
Wait, the rule says: "Counter triangle (a BONUS, not an automatic win): spearman counters knight; archer counters spearman; knight counters archer. Having the counter multiplies your WHOLE army's force by 1.5 (every troop in it, not only the countering type). The MOST NUMEROUS type of each army sets the counter matchup (ties in count break in the order spearman, archer, knight)."
So my most numerous is spearman (3). Defender has knight (1). Spearman counters knight. So my attack force is multiplied by 1.5.
My attack force: 7 * 1.5 = 10.5.
Defender's effective defense: 5.
So I win easily. But wait, "The LOSER is destroyed entirely - this includes an attacker that fails against a village. The WINNER loses troops worth HALF of the loser's effective force, spread over its troop types: that amount does not depend on the winner's size, so a bigger winning army loses a smaller share of itself."
If I attack Teruel, my force is 10.5, defense is 5. Loser is defender (force 5). Winner loses half of loser's effective force, which is 2.5. Spread over troop types. Defender has 1 archer, 1 knight. Half of their force is 2.5. My army loses some troops. But since I win, I take the village.

This is tricky. The counter triangle makes it very important to have the right troop composition.
Let's analyze the counter matchups:
To win an attack, I need my force > defender's effective force (with counter if applicable).
Let's write down the formulas:
My Attack Force = (sum of attacks) * (1.5 if I have counter)
Defender Defense Force = (effective defense) * (1.5 if defender has counter)

When does defender have counter?
Defender's most numerous type counters my most numerous type.
- If my most numerous is spearman, defender needs archer to counter.
- If my most numerous is archer, defender needs knight to counter.
- If my most numerous is knight, defender needs spearman to counter.

When do I have counter?
My most numerous type counters defender's most numerous type.
- If my most numerous is spearman, defender needs knight.
- If my most numerous is archer, defender needs spearman.
- If my most numerous is knight, defender needs archer.

Let's look at the targets:
1. Vigo [10]: 4 archers. Most numerous: archer.
   Effective defense: 10.
   To counter defender (archer), I need knight as my most numerous.
   To avoid defender countering me, my most numerous should NOT be spearman (since defender has archer, which counters spearman). But defender has no spearman, so if my most numerous is archer, defender has no knight to counter me. If my most numerous is knight, defender has no spearman to counter me.
   So if I attack with mostly knights, I get counter (knight counters archer), and defender doesn't counter me.
   Let's test: attack with knights.
   Attack per knight: 4.
   If I send N knights: attack = 4N. Counter: yes (knight vs archer). Force = 6N.
   Defender defense = 10. No counter for defender (archer vs knight? knight counters archer, so defender's archer does not counter my knight. Wait, "knight counters archer". So MY knight counters defender's archer. So I get the counter. Does defender's archer counter my knight? No, knight is not countered by archer. Archer counters spearman. So defender gets no counter).
   So defender force = 10.
   I need 6N > 10 => N >= 2.
   So 2 knights can beat Vigo!
   Attack of 2 knights = 8. With counter = 12. 12 > 10. Win.
   Do I have 2 knights at Porto [6]? Porto has 1 archer. I can send knights from Coimbra [3] (1 knight) and Lisboa [0] (1 knight) to Porto, but they take 1 turn to arrive. Then they need another turn to attack Vigo.
   So I cannot attack Vigo next turn with 2 knights unless they are already at Porto.
   Can I send 1 knight from Coimbra to Porto (arrives next turn), and 1 knight from Lisboa to Porto (arrives next turn)? Then next turn Porto has 2 knights (plus the 1 archer). Then the turn after (Turn 10), I can attack Vigo with 2 knights.
   Wait, if they arrive at Porto next turn, they are at Porto. To attack Vigo, they need to march from Porto to Vigo, which takes 1 turn. So they would attack Vigo on Turn 11.
   But I can also just build knights at Porto? Porto has wood 70, iron 50. Can build 1 knight (30 wood, 30 iron). So next turn Porto has 1 knight + 1 archer. Still not 2 knights.
   So attacking Vigo requires more preparation.

2. Cordoba [11]: 4 knights. Most numerous: knight.
   Effective defense: 10.
   To counter defender (knight), I need spearman as my most numerous.
   To avoid defender countering me, my most numerous should NOT be archer (since defender has knight, which counters archer).
   So if I attack with mostly spearmen, I get counter (spearman counters knight), and defender doesn't counter me (knight does not counter spearman).
   Let's test: attack with spearmen.
   Attack per spearman: 1.
   If I send N spearmen: attack = N. Counter: yes (spearman vs knight). Force = 1.5N.
   Defender defense = 10. No counter for defender (knight vs spearman? spearman counters knight, so defender's knight does not counter my spearman. Wait, "spearman counters knight". So MY spearman counters defender's knight. Defender gets no counter).
   So defender force = 10.
   I need 1.5N > 10 => N >= 7.
   So I need 7 spearmen to attack Cordoba!
   Do I have 7 spearmen? I have 5 spearmen total (2 at Salamanca, 3 at Toledo). Not enough.
   What if I attack with archers?
   Archer vs knight: knight counters archer. So defender gets counter!
   Defender force = 10 * 1.5 = 15.

   My attack = 4N. Defender defense = 10.

Let's summarize the plans for Turn 9 (the turn we are playing now):

- Build troops to maximize economy and army.

  Knight does NOT counter spearman. (Archer counters spearman, knight counters archer, spearman counters knight).
  So defender gets NO counter.
  Defender force = 10.
  My force = 10.5.
  10.5 > 10. I WIN!
  And the loser is defender (Cordoba). Winner loses half of loser's effective force = 5.
  Cordoba has 4 knights. Effective defense 10. Half is 5. So Cordoba loses some knights.
